Global Solutions For Systems of Quadratic Nonlinear Schrödinger Equations in 3D
Boyang Su

Abstract




1
Introduction

In this work, we prove global well-posedness and scattering for systems of quadratic nonlinear Schrödinger equations in the critical three-dimensional case, for small, localized data. For the terms corresponding to the nonlinearity
u\bar{u}
, we need to do an
\epsilon
regularization of this part of the nonlinearity.

In order to tackle quadratic space-time resonances, after performing a Littlewood-Paley decomposition, we use integration by parts in the Duhamel term, to take advantage of the oscillations when space-time resonances are absent.

1.1
Background

The existence of a global solution for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation

i\partial_{t}u+\Delta u=P(u),

(NLS)

where
u(t,x):\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
and
P
is a nonlinear homogeneous function, has been extensively studied. Many papers focused on the case of a gauge invariant nonlinearity, namely
P(u)=\lambda|u|^{p-1}u
for some real constant
p>1
and
\lambda\neq 0
, where solutions satisfy several conservation laws. In the
L^{2}
-subcritical cases, i.e.
1<p<1+\frac{4}{n}
, a global solution can be obtained by iterations of local well-posedness results. In the energy-subcritical case, i.e.
1<p<1+\frac{4}{n-2}
, the local result can also be extended globally in time with some smallness condition imposed on the initial data
[
14
]
.

The problem of global well-posedness and scattering for a general nonlinearity
P
, homogeneous of degree
p
, turns out to be more complicated, especially as
p
becomes smaller. An important notion related to this problem is the Strauss exponent
[
18
]
,

\frac{\sqrt{n^{2}+12n+4}+n+2}{2n},

derived from the positive root of
np^{2}-(n+2)p-2=0
. Notably, the Strauss exponent is smaller than the mass critical exponent,
1+\frac{4}{n}
. When
p
is above this Strauss exponent, we can get small data global well-posedness and scattering for (NLS) using a fixed point argument with dispersion properties of the solution to linear Schrödinger solution
[
18
]
. Some other works include Shatah
[
16
]
, Klainerman and Ponce
[
11
]
, and Kato
[
9
]
. The Strauss exponent serves as an essential concept within the broader scope of nonlinear dispersive equations. In particular, for the nonlinear wave equation, it is the positive root of
(n-1)p^{2}-(n+1)p-2=0
. For any
p
greater than this Strauss exponent, Lindblad and Sogge
[
15
]
proved global existence for small initial data with spherical symmetry, where the symmetry requirement can be removed when
n\leq 8
. Later in
[
3
]
, Georgiev, Lindblad, and Sogge removed the symmetry assumption for all dimensions.

In dimension
n=3
, the Strauss exponent for NLS is
2
, which makes quadratic NLS in 3D an interesting case to study. Adopting the vector field method in Klainerman
[
12
]
, one can show the existence of global solutions and scattering for a quadratic nonlinearity involving
u^{2}
and
\bar{u}^{2}
. Relevant works using this method include Hayashi
[
6
]
, Hayashi and Naumkin
[
7
]
, and Kawahara
[
10
]
. In
[
4
]
, Germain, Masmoudi, and Shatah developed a more transparent proof combining the vector field and normal forms methods to deal with the space-time resonance, which can be generalized to systems of quadratic NLS.

However, for the nonlinearity of form
u\bar{u}
, the space-time resonance is 3-dimensional and we only have almost global existence in Ginibre and Hayashi
[
5
]
and Wang
[
19
]
. Furthermore, Wang managed to prove the existence of global solutions when the quadratic term contains “
\epsilon
”derivatives in the low-frequency part. But global existence remains unknown for the general case of
|u|^{2}
. In
[
8
]
, Ikeda and Inui showed that the solution blows up in finite time if the initial data
|u_{0}(x)|\geq|x|^{-k}
for
|x|>1
and
3/2<k<2
. Hence, a necessary condition for the small data global existence in the
|u|^{2}
case is fast enough decay at spacial infinity of the initial data.

1.2
Main Result

This paper generalizes Germain, Masmoudi, and Shatah’s result
[
4
, Theorem 2]
on systems of quadratic nonlinear Schrödinger equations, to incorporate the quadratic resonance cases when
\frac{1}{c_{l}}=\frac{1}{c_{m}}+\frac{1}{c_{n}}
and when
c_{m}+c_{n}=0
. The case when
c_{m}+c_{n}=0
is analogous to the
u\bar{u}
nonlinearity, where we use Wang’s idea in
[
19
]
to add an “
\epsilon
”derivative to the low-frequency part of this quadratic nonlinear term.

We thus consider the system of quadratic nonlinear Schrödinger equations (see Section
1.3
for the unexplained notations)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u_{l}=ic_{l}\Delta u_{l}+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0}A_{lmn}u_{m}u_{n}+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}=0}A_{lmn}Q(u_{m},u_{n})\\
u_{l}|_{t=1}=u_{l0}\end{cases},

(1.1)

where
1\leq l,m,n\leq N
,
u_{l}(t,x):[1,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
u_{0}\in H^{10}
,
c_{l}
are nonzero real numbers, and

Q(u_{m},u_{n})=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{u}_{m}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{u}_{n}(t,\eta)d\eta,

for
q(\xi,\eta)=q(\eta,\xi)
. Without loss of generality, we assume
m\geq n
for all equations in (
1.1
) and the constants
A_{lmn}=A_{lnm}
. As in
[
19
]
, we also impose the following condition on the symbol of
Q
,

\displaystyle\sup_{k,k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\{\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}+

(1.2)

\displaystyle+2^{k}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\nabla_{\xi}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}+

\displaystyle+2^{2k}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\nabla^{2}_{\xi}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}+

\displaystyle+2^{\min\{k_{1},k_{2}\}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\nabla_{\eta}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}+

\displaystyle+2^{2\min\{k_{1},k_{2}\}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\nabla^{2}_{\eta}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}+

\displaystyle+2^{k+\min\{k_{1},k_{2}\}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\nabla_{\eta}\nabla_{\xi}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}\}\lesssim 2^{\epsilon k_{-}}.

Define

\|f\|_{Z}=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{-\gamma k+2k_{+}+k/2+\alpha k}\|D^{1+\alpha}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}},

(1.3)

where
f_{k}
is the Littlewood-Paley projection at frequence
2^{k}
and
\alpha,\gamma
are positive real constants satisfying

0<\gamma<\epsilon/3\ll 1

(1.4)

and

1/2-\gamma/8<\alpha<1/2-\gamma/(16-2\gamma).

(1.5)

The definition of
k_{\pm}
and Littlewood-Paley projection will be provided in Section
1.3
. Note that the weights in the
Z
norm guarantee
|x|^{-2}
decay as
|x|\rightarrow\infty
of the initial data. Using the Sobolev inequality, we have

\displaystyle\||x|^{1/2}f(x)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|D^{1/2}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\chi_{2^{k-2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{k}}\|_{L^{\frac{6}{1+2\alpha}}}\|D^{1/2}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5-2\alpha}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k/2+\alpha k}\|D^{1+\alpha}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}}\|f\|_{Z}\lesssim\|f\|_{Z}.

This rules out the blow-up possibility introduced by Ikeda and Inui
[
8
]
. Next, we state the main theorem, which proves global existence with scattering for (
1.1
) when the initial data is small and localized around the origin.

Theorem 1.1
.

Fix
\alpha
,
\gamma
and
\epsilon
. If (
1.4
) and (
1.5
) are satisfied, and for all
l

\|u_{l0}\|_{H^{10}}+\|e^{-ic_{l}\Delta}u_{l0}\|_{Z}\leq\epsilon_{0},

(1.6)

for a sufficiently small constant
\epsilon_{0}
, then there exists a unique global solution for the IVP (
1.1
). Moreover, for all
l
, the following estimate holds,

\sup_{t\in[1,\infty)}\|u_{l}(t,x)\|_{H^{10}_{x}}+\|e^{-ic_{l}t\Delta}u_{l}(t,x)\|_{Z_{x}}+t^{1+\gamma/2}\|u_{l}(t,x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

(1.7)

and the solution
u_{l}
scatters.

In
[
4
]
, Germain, Masmoudi, and Shatah proved global existence and scattering of small solutions to the system (
1.1
), assuming no quadratic resonance, i.e.
A_{lmn}=0
when
\frac{1}{c_{l}}=\frac{1}{c_{m}}+\frac{1}{c_{n}}
and when
c_{m}+c_{n}=0
. They defined the quadratic space-time resonance as
\mathcal{T}_{l,m,n}\cap\mathcal{S}_{m,n}
, where

\mathcal{T}_{l,m,n}=\{(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}:c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+c_{n}|\eta|^{2}=c_{l}|\xi+\eta|^{2}\}

is the time resonance and

\mathcal{S}_{m,n}=\{(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}:c_{m}\xi=c_{n}\eta\}

is the space resonance. No quadratic resonance corresponds to
\mathcal{T}_{l,m,n}\cap\mathcal{S}_{m,n}=\{(0,0)\}
. The nonlinear component in Duhamel’s formula for the profile
\hat{f}_{l}(t,\xi)=e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}\hat{u}_{l}(t,\xi)
is given by

\int_{1}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{is(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m}(s,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n}(s,\eta)d\eta ds.

Germain, Masmoudi, and Shatah used integration by parts in time and frequency to take advantage of the oscillation due to the fact of no quadratic resonance. However, when
\frac{1}{c_{l}}=\frac{1}{c_{m}}+\frac{1}{c_{n}}
and when
c_{m}+c_{n}=0
, the dimension of space-time resonance goes up to three, making it hard to apply the previous method.

It is worth noting that
c_{m}=1,c_{n}=-1
contains the case of
u\bar{u}
nonlinearity. Wang
[
19
]
suggested using Littlewood-Paley to decompose the frequency space, enabling one to conduct a more detailed study to fully exploit the interplay between the time variable and the frequency levels.

When one attempts to use Wang’s method for the case of
u^{2}
, the order of derivatives in Wang’s norm
\|\nabla^{2}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}
is too large and one cannot obtain enough decay in the time variable for the estimations. Interestingly, there is a term in the norm in
[
4
]
with the same weights, namely
\|\frac{x^{2}}{\sqrt{t}}f(t,x)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
. Nevertheless, the presence of
\sqrt{t}
in the denominator effectively counteracts the substantial growth in the time variable caused by these weights.

Subsequently, work by Léger
[
13
]
demonstrated, in particular, small data global existence and scattering for NLS with
u^{2}
nonlinearity, employing the norm
\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}}
. Léger’s work served as the inspiration to lower the order of derivatives in Wang’s norm. However, in the
u\bar{u}
case, the oscillation in the Duhamel’s formula, taking the form of
e^{i2s\xi\cdot\eta}
, does not provide enough decay when focusing on the lower frequencies. Hence, the
t^{-3/2}
decay brought by the
e^{it\Delta}:L^{1}\rightarrow L^{\infty}
estimate played a crucial role in this case. The weights required for bounding the
L^{1}
norm of
f_{k}
translate into at least
3/2
derivatives in
\hat{f}_{k}
, leading to the
D^{1+\alpha}
derivatives in our
Z
norm. Nevertheless, taking
\alpha\geq 1/2
will result in excessive powers of
t
that we cannot control, so we required
\alpha<1/2
.

Next, we briefly outline the proof and the structure of this paper.

Assuming the initial condition (
1.6
), we know by the local well-posedness (see Proposition
5.1
) that there exists some
T>1
so that there is a unique solution
\{u_{l}\}
to (
1.1
) such that

\sup_{t\in[1,T]}\|u_{l}(t,x)\|_{H^{10}_{x}}+\|e^{-ic_{l}t\Delta}u_{l}(t,x)\|_{Z_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{1}:=\epsilon_{0}^{5/6}.

(1.8)

We use this as the bootstrap assumption and Proposition
4.1
shows that under (
1.8
)

\sup_{t\in[1,T]}\|u_{l}(t,x)\|_{H^{10}_{x}}+\|e^{-ic_{l}t\Delta}u_{l}(t,x)\|_{Z_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

which leads to a global solution for (
1.1
) and this solution also scatters and decays in time.

The detailed proof of Theorem
1.1
is presented in Section
4
, while Section
5
contains the proof of local well-posedness and Section
6
provides the detailed estimates for the bootstrap argument (Proposition
4.1
). For the reader’s convenience, we included some standard inequalities in Section
2
. Furthermore, Section
3
provides proofs for some estimates utilized in Section
6
.

1.3
Notations

We will introduce some notations used in this paper. Let

\mathcal{F}f=\hat{f}(\xi)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-ix\cdot\xi}f(x)dx

and

\mathcal{F}^{-1}f=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}f(\xi)d\xi

denote Fourier transfrom and inverse Fourier transform. In particular,

\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\|_{L^{1}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi+iy\cdot\eta}m(\xi,\eta)d\xi d\eta\bigg|dxdy.

For the Littlewood-Paley operator, we choose an increasing smooth function
\Psi:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
0\leq\Psi(x)\leq 1
,
\Psi(x)=0
on
\{|x|<1/2\}
, and
\Psi(x)=1
on
\{|x|\geq 1\}
. Then,
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined as
\psi(x)=\Psi(2|x|)-\Psi(|x|)
is also smooth, nonnegative and supported on
\{1/4\leq|x|\leq 1\}
. Let
\psi_{k}(x)=\psi(x/2^{k})
, we get

1\equiv\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{k}(x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Psi(|x|/2^{k-1})-\Psi(|x|/2^{k}).

Hence,
\psi_{k}
forms a partition of unity on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. For an integer
a\geq 3
to be determined later in (
3.16
), define another Schwartz function
\tilde{\psi}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow[0,1]
supported on
\{2^{-2a-1}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{a+3}\}
and
\tilde{\psi}(\xi)=1
for
2^{-2a}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{a+2}
, so that
\psi(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(\xi)=\psi(\xi)
. Let
\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)=\tilde{\psi}(\xi/2^{k})
, then we have
\psi_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)=\psi_{k}(\xi)
and we define

f_{k}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\hat{f}(\xi)\psi_{k}(\xi).

The linear Schrödinger equation operator is defined as follows

e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}u_{0}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}e^{-ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}\hat{u}_{0}(\xi).

The fractional Sobolev spaces are defined as

H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})=\{f\in\mathcal{S^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}):\Lambda^{s}f(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}((1+|\xi|^{2})^{s/2}\hat{f}(\xi))(x)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\}

and fractional derivatives

D^{s}f=\mathcal{F}^{-1}|\xi|^{s}\hat{f}(\xi).

For any spacial norm
X
, the mixed norm is defined as

\|f(t,x)\|_{L^{p}_{t}([a,b])X_{x}}=\begin{cases}\bigg(\int_{a}^{b}\|f(t,x)\|_{X_{x}}^{p}dt\bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}},&1\leq p<\infty\\
\sup_{t\in[a,b]}\|f(t,x)\|_{X_{x}},&p=\infty\end{cases},

Lastly, in this paper, we denote

k_{-}=\min\{k,0\},\,k_{+}=\max\{k,0\},

the phase function

\phi(\xi,\eta)=c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2},

and

\chi^{1}_{k}=\{(k_{1},k_{2}):|k-k_{1}|\leq 3,k_{2}\leq k_{1}-a\}
,

\chi^{2}_{k}=\{(k_{1},k_{2}):|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a,k<k_{1}-a-2\}
and
\chi^{3}_{k}=\{(k_{1},k_{2}):|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a,|k-k_{1}|\leq a+2\}
, with
a
as above.

2
Background Estimates

In this section, we present several fundamental results concerning the Littlewood-Paley decomposition, fractional derivatives, linear Schrödinger equation, and bilinear estimations. These results will play a crucial role in the subsequent proofs, providing essential tools and techniques for our estimations.

2.1
Tools for Fractional Derivatives

The definition of the
Z
norm involves the
L^{2}
norm of fractional derivatives, hence we will present two interpolation-type results. Using these lemmas, we may obtain a bound for the fractional derivative using the bounds we found for the integer order derivatives. The first one below bounds the
\alpha
fractional derivative by the function and its first derivative.

Lemma 2.1
.

For any
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, we have

\displaystyle\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha},

where
\alpha\in[0,1]
.

Proof.

This is a direct result of Hölder’s inequality and Plancherel’s theorem,

\displaystyle\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}}\leq\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\xi|^{2\alpha}|\hat{f}|^{2}d\xi\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\xi|^{2\alpha}|\hat{f}|^{2\alpha})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}d\xi\bigg)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|\hat{f}|^{2-2\alpha})^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}d\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}

\displaystyle=\|\xi\hat{f}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|\hat{f}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}=\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}

∎

However, we will not always have the
1-\alpha
extra derivative that we may use to bound the
\alpha
derivative. In those cases, we use a variation of a special case of the Stein’s interpolation theorem
[
17
, Theorem 4.1]
tailored for the specific problem setting in this paper.

The lemma below presents the two interpolation results, whose domains of the family of operators are different. The first one is a result on space norm, while the second one involves mixed space-time norm. The proof is analogous to Stein’s proof and is based on the three lines lemma
[
14
, Lemma 2.1]
.

Lemma 2.2

(Interpolation)
.

Let
S=\{x+iy\in\mathbb{C}:0\leq x\leq 1\}
and
\{T_{z}\}_{z\in S}
be a family of linear operators such that
(T_{z}f)g
is integrable whenever
f\in X
and
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Furthermore,

z\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(T_{z}f)g

is analytic in the interior of
S
, continuous and uniformly bounded on
S
. Take
0\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}
.

(a)

When
X=C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, if

\displaystyle\|T_{iy}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{x}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|T_{1+iy}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{1}\|f\|_{H^{s_{2}}_{x}},

(2.1)

for any
y\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\displaystyle\|T_{\theta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim M_{0}^{1-\theta}M_{1}^{\theta}\|f\|_{H^{(1-\theta)s_{1}+\theta s_{2}}_{x}},

for all
0\leq\theta\leq 1
.

(b)

When
X=L^{\infty}[1,T]C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, if

\displaystyle\|T_{iy}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{0}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{s_{1}}_{x}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|T_{1+iy}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{1}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{s_{2}}_{x}},

(2.2)

for any
y\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\displaystyle\|T_{\theta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim M_{0}^{1-\theta}M_{1}^{\theta}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{(1-\theta)s_{1}+\theta s_{2}}_{x}},

for all
0\leq\theta\leq 1
.

Proof.

We first show part
(a)
. Define a new family of operators for
z\in S
,

T^{\prime}_{z}=T_{z}\Lambda^{-s_{1}+(s_{1}-s_{2})z},

where
\Lambda^{s}f(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{s/2}\hat{f}(\xi).

Then, as a result of (
2.1
),

\displaystyle\|T^{\prime}_{iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\|T_{iy}\Lambda^{-s_{1}+(s_{1}-s_{2})iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{0}\|\Lambda^{-s_{1}+(s_{1}-s_{2})iy}g\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{x}}=M_{0}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

and

\displaystyle\|T^{\prime}_{1+iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\|T_{1+iy}\Lambda^{-s_{1}+(s_{1}-s_{2})(1+iy)}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{1}\|\Lambda^{-s_{2}+(s_{1}-s_{2})iy}g\|_{H^{s_{2}}_{x}}=M_{1}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

For
g,h\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
\|g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=1
, define

\Psi(z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(T^{\prime}_{z}g)(x)h(x)dx.

Then the condition on
T_{z}
implies
\Psi(z)
is analytic in the interior of
S
, and continuous and uniformly bounded on
S
. From Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

|\Psi(iy)|\leq\|T^{\prime}_{iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{0}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=M_{0}

and

|\Psi(1+iy)|\leq\|T^{\prime}_{1+iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{1}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=M_{1}.

Hence, three lines theorem
[
14
, Lemma 2.1]
implies for any
\theta\in[0,1]

|\Psi(\theta)|\leq M^{1-\theta}_{0}M^{\theta}_{1}.

Using duality and the density of
C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, we get
T^{\prime}_{\theta}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
is bounded with norm
M^{1-\theta}_{0}M^{\theta}_{1}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\|T_{\theta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=\|T^{\prime}_{\theta}\Lambda^{s_{1}-(s_{1}-s_{2})\theta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim M_{0}^{1-\theta}M_{1}^{\theta}\|\Lambda^{s_{1}-(s_{1}-s_{2})\theta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=M_{0}^{1-\theta}M_{1}^{\theta}\|f\|_{H^{s_{1}-(s_{1}-s_{2})\theta}_{x}}.

For part (b), we use the same
T^{\prime}_{z}
as above.
From (
2.2
), we get

\displaystyle\|T^{\prime}_{iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\|T_{iy}\Lambda^{-s_{1}+(s_{1}-s_{2})iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{0}\|\Lambda^{-s_{1}+(s_{1}-s_{2})iy}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{s_{1}}_{x}}=M_{0}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{x}}

and

\displaystyle\|T^{\prime}_{1+iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\|T_{1+iy}\Lambda^{-s_{2}+(s_{1}-s_{2})iy}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{1}\|\Lambda^{-s_{2}+(s_{1}-s_{2})iy}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{s_{2}}_{x}}=M_{1}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{x}}.

Then, it suffices to show the interpolation result,
T^{\prime}_{\theta}:L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{x}\rightarrow L^{2}_{x}
is bounded such that

\displaystyle\|T^{\prime}_{\theta}g\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M^{1-\theta}_{0}M^{\theta}_{1}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{x}},

(2.3)

for any
0\leq\theta\leq 1
, since (
2.3
) gives

\displaystyle\|T_{\theta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=

\displaystyle\|T^{\prime}_{\theta}\Lambda^{s_{1}-(s_{1}-s_{2})\theta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq M_{0}^{1-\theta}M_{1}^{\theta}\|\Lambda^{s_{1}-(s_{1}-s_{2})\theta}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{x}}=M_{0}^{1-\theta}M_{1}^{\theta}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{s_{1}-(s_{1}-s_{2})\theta}_{x}}.

Now, we are left to prove (
2.3
).

Take
g\in L_{t}^{\infty}([1,T])C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
h\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
so that
\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{x}}=\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=1
. Define
\Psi(z)
same as above, we get

|\Psi(iy)|\leq M_{0}

and

|\Psi(1+iy)|\leq M_{1}
.
Using three lines theorem, we have for any
\theta\in[0,1]

\displaystyle|\Psi(\theta)|\leq M_{0}^{1-\theta}M_{1}^{\theta},

which shows (
2.3
) by duality.
∎

2.2
Bilinear Estimates

Since we have the quadratic nonlinear terms in (
1.1
), using Duhamel’s principle will result in bilinear integral forms. The lemma below provides us with an estimate for such type of integral forms with a multiplier
m
.

Lemma 2.3
.

For
1\leq p,q,r\leq\infty
,
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and
g\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. If

\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\|_{L^{1}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi+iy\cdot\eta}m(\xi,\eta)d\eta d\xi\bigg|dxdy<\infty,

then the following bilinear estimates hold

(a)

\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\eta)d\eta\|_{L^{r}}\leq\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{p}}\|g\|_{L^{q}}
,

(b)

\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\xi)d\xi\|_{L^{r}}\leq\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{p}}\|g\|_{L^{q}}
,

where
1/r=1/p+1/q
.

Proof.

Take an arbitrary test function
\varphi(x)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that
\|\varphi(x)\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}}\leq 1
with
1/r+1/r^{\prime}=1
. Suppose
f,g\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, then we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi(x)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\eta)d\eta dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}\varphi(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\eta)d\eta d\xi dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}\varphi(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)e^{-iz\cdot(\xi-\eta)}f(z)e^{-iy\cdot\eta}g(y)dzdyd\eta d\xi dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{i(x-z)\cdot\xi+i(z-y)\cdot\eta}m(\xi,\eta)d\eta d\xi\varphi(x)f(z)g(y)dydxdz.

Perform a change of variable on
x
and
y
. In particular, we take
\tilde{x}=x-z
and
\tilde{y}=z-y
. Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi(x)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\eta)d\eta dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{i\tilde{x}\cdot\xi+i\tilde{y}\cdot\eta}m(\xi,\eta)d\eta d\xi\varphi(\tilde{x}+z)f(z)g(z-\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}M(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\varphi(\tilde{x}+z)f(z)g(z-\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}dz,

where

M(x,y)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi+iy\cdot\eta}m(\xi,\eta)d\eta d\xi.

Using Hölder’s in equality and Minkowski’s integral inequality, for
1/p+1/p^{\prime}=1
and
1/p^{\prime}=1/r^{\prime}+1/q
, we get

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi(x)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\eta)d\eta dx\bigg|\leq

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}M(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\varphi(\tilde{x}+z)g(z-\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}f(z)dz\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}M(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\varphi(\tilde{x}+z)g(z-\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}\bigg\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{z}}\|f\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|M(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|\|\varphi(\tilde{x}+z)g(z-\tilde{y})\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{z}}d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}\|f\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|M(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|\|\varphi\|_{L^{r^{\prime}}}\|g\|_{L^{q}}d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}\|f\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|M(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\|_{L^{1}}\|g\|_{L^{q}}\|f\|_{L^{p}},

which shows
(a)
by duality. Then, we can use the density of Schwartz space in
L^{p}
to extend the result to any
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
g\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
1\leq p,q<\infty
. For the case of
L^{\infty}
, we can assume without loss of generality
g\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and perform approximation using
g_{n}(x)=g(x)\chi_{|x|\leq n}(x)
.

The proof for statement
(b)
is analogous. Starting with the test function
\varphi
,
f
, and
g
in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi(x)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\xi)d\xi dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\eta}\varphi(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\xi)d\xi d\eta dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\eta}\varphi(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)e^{-iz\cdot(\xi-\eta)}f(z)e^{-iy\cdot\xi}g(y)dzdyd\xi d\eta dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{i(-y-z)\cdot\xi+i(x+z)\cdot\eta}m(\xi,\eta)d\eta d\xi\varphi(x)f(z)g(y)dydxdz.

Using
\tilde{x}=x+z
and
\tilde{y}=-y-z
for change of variables, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi(x)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\xi)d\xi dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{i\tilde{y}\cdot\xi+i\tilde{x}\cdot\eta}m(\xi,\eta)d\eta d\xi\varphi(\tilde{x}-z)f(z)g(-\tilde{y}-z)d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}M(\tilde{y},\tilde{x})\varphi(\tilde{x}-z)f(z)g(-\tilde{y}-z)d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}dz.

Lastly, we obtain (b) from Hölder’s in equality and Minkowski’s integral inequality,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi(x)\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}(\eta)d\eta dx\bigg|\leq

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}M(\tilde{y},\tilde{x})\varphi(\tilde{x}-z)g(-\tilde{y}-z)d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}f(z)dz\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|M(\tilde{y},\tilde{x})|\|\varphi(\tilde{x}-z)g(-\tilde{y}-z)\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{z}}d\tilde{y}d\tilde{x}\|f\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|M(\tilde{y},\tilde{x})\|_{L^{1}}\|g\|_{L^{q}}\|f\|_{L^{p}},

and extend the result to
L^{p}
spaces.
∎

The next result is an immediate application of the bilinear estimates above, combined with a duality argument. Here, we chose to estimate the term
\|e^{it\Delta}f_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}
by Bernstein’s inequality (see Lemma
2.11
),

\|e^{it\Delta}f_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3k/2}\|e^{it\Delta}f_{k}\|_{L^{2}}=\|f_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

However, as we will see later, there are instances where we utilize the properties of the linear Schrödinger equation to obtain different bounds, for example,
\|e^{it\Delta}f_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim t^{-3/2}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{1}}
. The key advantage of Lemma
2.4
becomes evident when the magnitude of
2^{k}
is relatively small compared to
t^{-1}
.

Lemma 2.4
.

For any
k,k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|f_{k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

Using Plancherel’s theorem and the bilinear estimate
L^{2}\times L^{\infty}\rightarrow L^{2}
in Lemma
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\hat{f}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}_{k_{2}}(\xi)d\eta\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|f_{k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{k_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{3k_{2}/2}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|f_{k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}},

where the last step follows from Bernstein’s inequality in Lemma
2.11
.

On the other hand, we use duality to estimate the
L^{2}
norm. Take
h\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
\|h\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
and we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\hat{g}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta h(\xi)d\xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)h(\xi)d\xi\hat{g}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\hat{f}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)h(\xi)d\xi\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\bigg\|_{L^{2}}\|\hat{g}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\hat{f}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)h(\xi)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\xi\bigg\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|f_{k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}h(\xi)\psi_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g_{k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|f_{k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h(\xi)\psi_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{1}}\|g_{k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|f_{k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{3k/2}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|f_{k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}},

which finishes the proof.
∎

2.3
Multiplier Estimates

Recall the bilinear estimates in Lemma
2.3
. The
L^{1}
norm of the inverse Fourier transform of the multiplier shows up on the right-hand side. In this section, we present the
L^{1}
norm bounds on three different types of multipliers that we will encounter later in the main proof.

Lemma 2.5
.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. Suppose
\tau:\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is homogeneous of degree
n
and smooth on
\{(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}:\xi\neq 0,\xi-\eta\neq 0\}
. If
|k_{1}-k|=O(1)
, then

\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau(\xi,\eta)m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}^{2}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}^{2}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{nk}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

Proof.

Note that
\tau(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Using Young’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau(\xi,\eta)m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}^{2}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}^{2}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]*\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}2^{nk}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau(\xi/2^{k},\eta/2^{k})\tilde{\psi}(\xi/2^{k})\tilde{\psi}(2^{k-k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)/2^{k})]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}2^{nk}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(2^{k-k_{1}}(\xi-\eta))]\|_{L^{1}}.

The last line follows from the scale-invariant property
\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}D_{\delta}f(x)\|_{L^{1}}=\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}f(x)\|_{L^{1}}
, where
D_{\delta}f(x)=f(x/\delta)
. Then we are left to show,

\sup_{|k-k_{1}|=O(1)}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(2^{k-k_{1}}(\xi-\eta))]\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 1.

Take an arbitrary integer
|p|=O(1)
, define
f(\xi,\eta)=\tau(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(2^{p}(\xi-\eta))
. It suffices to show
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

We know
\tilde{\psi}(\xi)
is supported on
\{(\xi,\eta):2^{-2a-1}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{a+3}\}
and
\tilde{\psi}(2^{p}(\xi-\eta))
is supported on
\{(\xi,\eta):2^{-2a-1-p}\leq|\xi-\eta|\leq 2^{a+3-p}\}
. Hence,

supp\,f\subset\{(\xi,\eta):2^{-2a-1}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{a+3},2^{-2a-1-p}\leq|\xi-\eta|\leq 2^{a+3-p}\}\subset\{(\xi,\eta):|\eta|\leq 2^{a+4},|\xi|\leq 2^{a+3}\}.

Thus,
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
, since
\tilde{\psi},\tau
are smooth on the support of
f
. Also,
f
has compact support, so
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
.
∎

Lemma 2.6
.

Suppose
\tau_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is homogeneous of degree
n_{1}
,
\tau_{2}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is homogeneous of degree
n_{2}
, and
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
are both smooth on
\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
, then

\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(\xi)\tau_{2}(\eta)m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}^{2}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}^{2}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{n_{1}k+n_{2}k_{2}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}.

Proof.

Again, we use Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(\xi)\tau_{2}(\eta)m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}^{2}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}^{2}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]*\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(\xi)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[m(\xi,\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(\xi)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(\xi)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{iy\cdot\eta}\tau_{1}(\xi)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\eta d\xi\bigg|dxdy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}\tau_{1}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)d\xi\bigg|\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iy\cdot\eta}\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\eta\bigg|dydx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}\tau_{1}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)d\xi\bigg|dx\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iy\cdot\eta}\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\eta\bigg|dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)]\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{n_{1}k+n_{2}k_{2}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(\xi/2^{k})\tilde{\psi}(\xi/2^{k})]\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{2}(\eta/2^{k_{2}})\tilde{\psi}(\eta/2^{k_{2}})]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{n_{1}k+n_{2}k_{2}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(\xi)]\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{n_{1}k+n_{2}k_{2}},

where the last line follows from
\tau_{1}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(\xi),\,\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\eta)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. We know

\tau_{1}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(\xi)
and
\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\eta)
are both compactly supported on
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:2^{-2a-1}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{a+3}\}
, and smooth on their support.
∎

Lemma 2.7
.

Suppose
\tau_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is homogeneous of degree
n_{1}
,
\tau_{2}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is homogeneous of degree
n_{2}
, and
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
are both smooth on
\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
, then

\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(2c_{m}(\xi-\eta)-2c_{n}\eta)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(2c_{m}(\xi-\eta)-2c_{n}\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{n_{1}k_{1}+n_{2}k_{2}},

(2.4)

for
c_{m}\neq 0
.

Proof.

By a change of variable
\zeta=2c_{m}(\xi-\eta)-2c_{n}\eta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\tau_{1}(2c_{m}(\xi-\eta)-2c_{n}\eta)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(2c_{m}(\xi-\eta)-2c_{n}\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)]\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{iy\cdot\eta}\tau_{1}(2c_{m}(\xi-\eta)-2c_{n}\eta)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(2c_{m}(\xi-\eta)-2c_{n}\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\xi d\eta\bigg|dxdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ix\cdot(\frac{\zeta}{2c_{m}}+\frac{c_{n}}{c_{m}}\eta+\eta)}e^{iy\cdot\eta}\tau_{1}(\zeta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\zeta)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\zeta d\eta\bigg|dxdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{i(\frac{c_{n}}{c_{m}}x+x+y)\cdot\eta}e^{ix\cdot\frac{\zeta}{2c_{m}}}\tau_{1}(\zeta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\zeta)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\zeta d\eta\bigg|dxdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{i(\frac{c_{n}}{c_{m}}x+x+y)\cdot\eta}\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\eta\bigg|dy\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{i\frac{x}{2c_{m}}\cdot\zeta}\tau_{1}(\zeta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\zeta)d\zeta\bigg|dx.

Then, let
z_{1}=\frac{c_{n}}{c_{m}}x+x+y
,
z_{2}=\frac{x}{2c_{m}}
and use the homogeneity of
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|F^{-1}\tau_{1}(\xi-\eta)\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iz_{1}\cdot\eta}\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)d\eta\bigg|dz_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iz_{2}\cdot\zeta}\tau_{1}(\zeta)\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\zeta)d\zeta\bigg|dz_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iz_{1}\cdot\eta}\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\eta/2^{k_{2}})d\eta\bigg|dz_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iz_{2}\cdot\zeta}\tau_{1}(\zeta)\tilde{\psi}(\zeta/2^{k_{1}})d\zeta\bigg|dz_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{n_{1}k_{1}+n_{2}k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iz_{1}\cdot\eta}\tau_{2}(\eta/2^{k_{2}})\tilde{\psi}(\eta/2^{k_{2}})d\eta\bigg|dz_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iz_{2}\cdot\zeta}\tau_{1}(\zeta/2^{k_{1}})\tilde{\psi}(\zeta/2^{k_{1}})d\zeta\bigg|dz_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{n_{1}k_{1}+n_{2}k_{2}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}D_{2^{k_{1}}}(\tau_{1}\tilde{\psi})\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}D_{2^{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2}\tilde{\psi})\|_{L^{1}},

where the operator
D_{\delta}
denotes dilation, i.e.
D_{\delta}\psi(x)=\psi(x/\delta)
. Employing the dilation invariant property
\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}D_{\delta}\psi(x)\|_{L^{1}}=\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\psi(x)\|_{L^{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}D_{2^{k_{1}}}(\tau_{1}\tilde{\psi})\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}D_{2^{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2}\tilde{\psi})\|_{L^{1}}=\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\tau_{1}\tilde{\psi})\|_{L^{1}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\tau_{2}\tilde{\psi})\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 1.

This follows from
\tau_{1}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(\xi),\,\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\eta)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, since

\tau_{1}(\xi)\tilde{\psi}(\xi)
and
\tau_{2}(\eta)\tilde{\psi}(\eta)
are both compactly supported and smooth on their support. Thus, we showed (
2.4
).
∎

2.4
Linear Schrödinger Equation

The lemma below is a well-known result for the group of operators
\{e^{it\Delta}\}_{t=-\infty}^{\infty}
, which gives the solution to the linear initial value problem

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u=i\Delta u\\
u(x,0)=u_{0}\end{cases},

when applied to the initial data
u_{0}
. Using the fact that

e^{it\Delta}u_{0}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}e^{-it|\xi|^{2}}\hat{u}_{0}(\xi)=\frac{e^{-|x|^{2}/4it}}{(4it\pi)^{3/2}}*u_{0},

we observe that Plancherel’s theorem implies that
e^{it\Delta}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
is an isometry. Furthermore, the convolution form, in combination with Young’s inequality, shows that
e^{it\Delta}:L^{1}\rightarrow L^{\infty}
. By interpolation between these two bounds, we obtain the properties of the operator on the
L^{q}
spaces for
q\in[1,2]
.

Lemma 2.8
.

If
t\neq 0
,
1/p+1/q=1
, and
q\in[1,2]
, then
e^{it\Delta}:L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\rightarrow L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
is continuous and

\|e^{it\Delta}f\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim|t|^{-3/2(1/q-1/p)}\|f\|_{L^{q}}.

(2.5)

Proof.

See
[
14
, Lemma 4.1]
.
∎

Note that in this paper, we only consider
t\geq 1
to avoid singularity. This estimate will be used to obtain decay in the time variable in the later proofs. Another important property of the group
\{e^{it\Delta}\}_{t=-\infty}^{\infty}
is its global smoothing effect, namely the Strichartz’s estimates. Before we state the inequality, we need to introduce the definition of an admissible pair in our case when the dimension
n=3
.

Definition 2.1

(admissible pair)
.

We say
(q,r)
is admissible, if

\frac{2}{q}=3\bigg(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{r}\bigg)

and
2\leq r\leq 6
.

The Strichartz’s estimates encompass a family of results. Here, we will focus on stating the specific inequality that is relevant to this paper.

Lemma 2.9

(Strichartz’s estimate)
.

Let
I
be an interval of
\mathbb{R}
and
t_{0}\in\bar{I}
. If
(\gamma,\rho)
is an admissible pair and
f\in L_{t}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(I)L_{x}^{\rho^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, then for every admissible pair
(q,r)
, there exists a constant
C
independent of
I
such that

\bigg\|\int_{t_{0}}^{t}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}f(s)ds\bigg\|_{L^{q}_{t}(I)L_{x}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{\gamma^{\prime}}_{t}(I)L^{\rho^{\prime}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Proof.

See
[
2
, Theorem 2.3.3]
.
∎

Using the Strichartz’s estimate above, we may obtain a new type of bilinear estimate.

Lemma 2.10
.

For any
2^{M-1}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq 2^{M}
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f}(t,\xi-\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g}(t,\eta)d\eta dt\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-M/4}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|\hat{f}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([2^{M-1},2^{M}])H^{1}_{\xi}}\|\hat{g}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([2^{M-1},2^{M}])L^{2}_{\xi}}.

Proof.

Let
F(t,x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f}(t,\xi-\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g}(t,\eta)d\eta
. We rewrite the left hand side and use Plancherel’s theorem,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f}(t,\xi-\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g}(t,\eta)d\eta dt\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}F(t,x)dt\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t}e^{ic_{l}(t-s)\Delta}e^{-ic_{l}(t-s)\Delta}F(s,x)ds\bigg\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([t_{1},t_{2}])L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t}e^{ic_{l}(t-s)\Delta}e^{ic_{l}s\Delta}F(s,x)ds\bigg\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([t_{1},t_{2}])L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Since
(\infty,2)
and
(4,3)
are admissible pairs, we obtain from Strichartz’s estimate,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f}(t,\xi-\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g}(t,\eta)d\eta dt\bigg\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{l}s\Delta}F(s,x)\|_{L_{s}^{4/3}([t_{1},t_{2}])L^{3/2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Then, plugging in the definition of
F
and using the bilinear estimate
L^{6}\times L^{2}\rightarrow L^{3/2}
give

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{l}s\Delta}F(s,x)\|_{L_{s}^{4/3}([t_{1},t_{2}])L^{3/2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f}(t,\xi-\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g}(t,\eta)d\eta\bigg\|_{L_{t}^{4/3}([t_{1},t_{2}])L^{3/2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f}(t,\xi-\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g}(t,\eta)d\eta\bigg\|_{L_{t}^{4/3}([t_{1},t_{2}])L^{3/2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\big\|\|e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f(t,x)\|_{L_{x}^{6}}\|e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g(t,x)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\big\|_{L^{4/3}_{t}([t_{1},t_{2}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f(t,x)\|_{L_{t}^{4/3}([t_{1},t_{2}])L_{x}^{6}}\|g(t,x)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([t_{1},t_{2}])L_{x}^{2}}.

Lastly,
[
2
, Corollary 2.5.4]
gives

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f(t,x)\|_{L_{t}^{4/3}([t_{1},t_{2}])L_{x}^{6}}\lesssim\|t^{-1}\|_{L_{t}^{4/3}([t_{1},t_{2}])}\|\hat{f}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([t_{1},t_{2}])H^{1}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{-M/4}\|\hat{f}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([t_{1},t_{2}])H^{1}_{\xi}}.

∎

We compare this method with simply taking the
L^{\infty}
norm in
t
and applying the bilinear estimate
L^{\infty}\times L^{2}\rightarrow L^{2}
in Lemma
2.3
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f}(t,\xi-\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g}(t,\eta)d\eta dt\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{M}\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f}(t,\xi-\eta)\widehat{e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}g}(t,\eta)d\eta\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([2^{M-1},2^{M}])L^{2}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{M}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f(t,x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([2^{M-1},2^{M}])L_{x}^{\infty}}\|g(t,x)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([2^{M-1},2^{M}])L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-M/2}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}m(\xi,\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\|f(t,x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([2^{M-1},2^{M}])L_{x}^{1}}\|g(t,x)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([2^{M-1},2^{M}])L_{x}^{2}}.

This other method provides a greater decay in the time variable,
t\sim 2^{M}
. However, bounding the
L^{1}
norm of
f
also requires more derivatives than
\|\hat{f}\|_{H^{1}_{\xi}}
, which we will see in the next section.

2.5
Littlewood-Paley Operator

One may recall the definition of
Z
norm in (
1.3
), the frequency space is decomposed into dyadic annuli by the Littlewood-Paley operator. In this section, we will present some useful estimates as results of this decomposition. Bernstein’s inequality below is one basic result due to the localization in frequency space.

Lemma 2.11

(Bernstein’s inequality)
.

For any
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
,

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}}\lesssim 2^{3k(1/p-1/q)}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}.

Proof.

Since
\hat{f}_{k}=\hat{f}\psi_{k}=\hat{f}_{k}\tilde{\psi}_{k}
, where
\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)=\tilde{\psi}(\xi/2^{k})
is smooth and compactly supported, by Young’s convolution inequality,

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}}=\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)*f_{k}\|_{L^{q}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{\frac{1}{1-1/p+1/q}}}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}.

Then the computation

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\tilde{\psi}_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{\frac{1}{1-1/p+1/q}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it\xi\cdot x}\tilde{\psi}(\xi/2^{k})dx\bigg|^{\frac{1}{1-1/p+1/q}}dx\bigg)^{1-1/p+1/q}

\displaystyle=2^{3k}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\tilde{\psi}(2^{k}x)\bigg|^{\frac{1}{1-1/p+1/q}}dx\bigg)^{1-1/p+1/q}

\displaystyle=2^{3k}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\tilde{\psi}(y)\bigg|^{\frac{1}{1-1/p+1/q}}2^{-3k}dy\bigg)^{1-1/p+1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3k-3k(1-1/p+1/q)}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\tilde{\psi}\|_{L^{\frac{1}{1-1/p+1/q}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{3k(1/p-1/q)}

implies the desired estimate.

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}}\lesssim 2^{3k(1/p-1/q)}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}.

∎

In addition, the localization allows us to control the
L^{2}
norm of a function by the
L^{2}
norm of its derivative.

Lemma 2.12
.

For any
\hat{f}_{k}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
,

\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{k}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

(2.6)

Proof.

This is an immediate result of Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequality,

\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\chi_{2^{k-2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{k+1}}\|_{L^{3}}\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{6}}\lesssim 2^{k}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

∎

In order to use Lemma
2.8
, it is necessary to find a bound on
\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}
for
p\in[1,2]
. We may use Hölder’s inequality to control
\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}
with
p<2
by weighted
L^{2}
norms. Since derivatives on the Fourier side can be thought of as weights on the space side, we then use the bounds on
\|D^{\beta}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}
provided in the
Z
norm. This is shown in the result below.

Lemma 2.13
.

For any
\beta\geq 0
, if
p>\frac{6}{3+2\beta}
, then

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim 2^{-3k(1/p-1/2)+\beta k}\|D^{\beta}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

(2.7)

Furthermore, if
p>\frac{6}{5+2\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k+2k-3k/p}\|f\|_{Z},

(2.8)

and if
p>\frac{6}{3+2\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k+k-3k/p}\|f\|_{Z}.

(2.9)

Proof.

By Hölder’s inequality and Plancherel’s theorem,

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\int_{|x|\leq 2^{-k}}|f_{k}(x)|^{p}dx\bigg)^{1/p}+\bigg(\int_{|x|\geq 2^{-k}}|f_{k}(x)|^{p}dx\bigg)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\bigg(\int_{|x|\leq 2^{-k}}1dx\bigg)^{\frac{2-p}{2p}}+\bigg(\int_{|x|\geq 2^{-k}}|x|^{-\beta p}||x|^{\beta}f_{k}(x)|^{p}dx\bigg)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-3k(1/p-1/2)}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\||x|^{\beta}f_{k}(x)\|_{L^{2}}\bigg(\int_{|x|\geq 2^{-k}}|x|^{-\beta\frac{2p}{2-p}}dx\bigg)^{\frac{2-p}{2p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-3k(1/p-1/2)}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+2^{-3k(1/p-1/2)+\beta k}\|D^{\beta}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}},

the last line requires
p>\frac{6}{3+2\beta}
in order for the integral to converge.
Moreover, by Sobolev’s inequality

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{2}}=\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\chi_{2^{k-2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{k+2}}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{\beta}}}\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{\frac{6}{3-2\beta}}}\lesssim 2^{\beta k}\|D_{\xi}^{\beta}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-3k(1/p-1/2)+\beta k}\|D^{\beta}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

Thus, (
2.8
) is a direct consequence by taking
\beta=1+\alpha
and using the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{Z}
,

\displaystyle\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-3k(1/p-1/2)+(1+\alpha)k}\|D^{1+\alpha}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k+2k-3k/p}\|f\|_{Z}.

If we replace
f_{k}
by
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}
and let
\beta=\alpha
, we can obtain (
2.9
)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-3k(1/p-1/2)+\alpha k}\|D^{\alpha}_{\xi}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k+k-3k/p}\|f\|_{Z}.

∎

However, the lemma only gives us a bound on
\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}}
for
p>1
close to
1
. This is due to the constraint,
\alpha<1/2
, imposed on the term
\|D^{1+\alpha}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}
in
Z
norm.

3
Preliminary Estimates

3.1
Sobolev and
L^{p}
norms

There are places in Sections
2.1
and
2.4
, where we have to consider the Sobolev norms of
\hat{f}
. The
Z
norm is not sufficient to bound the Sobolev norms. However, after Littlewood-Paley decomposition, we can find a bound on the Sobolev norms of
\hat{f}_{k}
, given
f
bounded in the
H^{10}\cap Z
space.

Lemma 3.1
.

If
\|f\|_{H^{10}}+\|f\|_{Z}\leq a
, then for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{\alpha}}\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k+k/2-\alpha k}a,\,\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k/2}a,\,\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1+\alpha}}\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k/2-\alpha k}a.

(3.1)

In particular, the bootstrap assumption (
1.8
) implies

\|f_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\min\{2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}+k/2},2^{-10k_{+}}\}\epsilon_{1}.

(3.2)

Under the bootstrap assumption (
1.8
), we also have

\displaystyle\|D^{\alpha}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{\xi}}\leq\|\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{\alpha}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k+k/2-\alpha k}\epsilon_{1},

(3.3)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{\xi}}\leq\|\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{1}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k/2}\epsilon_{1},

\displaystyle\|D_{\xi}^{1+\alpha}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])L^{2}_{\xi}}\leq\|\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([1,T])H^{1+\alpha}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k/2-\alpha k}\epsilon_{1}.

Proof.

We start by showing for any
k\leq 0
and
s\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}(\xi)\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|D^{s}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}.

(3.4)

To prove (
3.4
), it suffices to show if
s_{1}\leq s_{2}
, then
\|D^{s_{1}}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|D^{s_{2}}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}
when
k\leq 0
.

\displaystyle\|D^{s_{1}}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\chi_{2^{k-2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{k+1}}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{s_{2}-s_{1}}}}\|D^{s_{1}}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{\frac{6}{3-2(s_{2}-s_{1})}}}\lesssim 2^{(s_{2}-s_{1})k}\|D^{s_{2}}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|D^{s_{2}}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}},

using Sobolev’s inequality with
s=s_{2}-s_{1}
and
p=\frac{6}{3-2(s_{2}-s_{1})}
.

Since
\|f\|_{H^{10}}+\|f\|_{Z}\leq a
, by the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{Z}
, we obtain the following
L^{2}
norm bound,

\|D_{\xi}^{1+\alpha}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}-k/2-\alpha k}a.

(3.5)

Then, Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequality imply

\|f_{l,k}(t,x)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\chi_{2^{k-2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{k+1}}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{1+\alpha}}_{\xi}}\|f_{l,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{\frac{6}{1-2\alpha}}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{k+\alpha k}\|D^{1+\alpha}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}+k/2}a

(3.6)

and

\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim\|\chi_{2^{k-2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{k+1}}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{\alpha}}_{\xi}}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{\frac{6}{3-2\alpha}}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{\alpha k}\|D^{1+\alpha}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}-k/2}a.

(3.7)

Since
\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}(t,x)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{-10k}\||\xi|^{10}e^{-ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}\hat{f}_{l}(t,\xi)\psi_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim 2^{-10k}\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l}(t,x)\|_{H^{10}_{x}},

\|f_{l,k}(t,x)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}(t,x)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{-10k}\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l}(t,x)\|_{H^{10}_{x}}\leq 2^{-10k}a.

(3.8)

Combine (
3.6
) and (
3.8
), we get

\|f_{l,k}(t,x)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\min\{2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}+k/2},2^{-10k_{+}}\}a,

(3.9)

and

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1}}\leq\|f_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\min\{2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}+k/2},2^{-10k_{+}}\}a+2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}-k/2}a\lesssim 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}-k/2}a.

Then we discuss the bound on
\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1+\alpha}}
in two cases.

When
k\leq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1+\alpha}}\lesssim\|D^{1+\alpha}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}-k/2-\alpha k}a,

by the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{Z}
.

When
k>0
, Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality in
[
1
]
gives,

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1+\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}},

so

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1}}^{1+\alpha}\lesssim\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{\alpha}\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1+\alpha}}\lesssim 2^{-10\alpha k}a^{\alpha}(\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1}}+\|D^{1+\alpha}\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}})\lesssim 2^{-10\alpha k+\gamma k-5k/2}a^{1+\alpha}.

Since
\alpha\in(1/3,1/2)
,

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim 2^{-10k+\gamma k+\frac{15k}{2(1+\alpha)}}a\leq 2^{\gamma k-5k/2-\alpha k}a\leq 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}-k/2-\alpha k}a.

Thus,

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1+\alpha}}\lesssim\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1}}+\|D^{1+\alpha}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}-k/2-\alpha k}a,

and by Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|\hat{f}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\alpha}\|\hat{f}_{k}\|_{H^{1}}^{\alpha}\lesssim 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}+k/2-\alpha k}a.

∎

In the next lemma, we combine the results in Lemma
2.13
and
3.1
to derive some bounds on the
L^{p}
norms of the solution
u_{l,k}
. Later these bounds will be used when we perform the
L^{2}\times L^{\infty}\rightarrow L^{2}
,
L^{4}\times L^{4}\rightarrow L^{2}
,
L^{3}\times L^{6}\rightarrow L^{2}
bilinear estimates.

Lemma 3.2
.

Under the assumption (
1.8
), for any
t\in[2^{M-1},2^{M}]

\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\min\{2^{-3M/2+\gamma M/8-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k+\gamma k/4},2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}+2k}\}\epsilon_{1},

(3.10)

\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-M/2+3k/2}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-M/2-2k_{+}+\gamma k+k}\epsilon_{1},

(3.11)

\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{3}}\lesssim 2^{-M/2+\gamma M/8-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k+\gamma k/4}\epsilon_{1},

(3.12)

and

\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim 2^{-3M/4+k/4}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-3M/4-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k/4}\epsilon_{1}.

(3.13)

Proof.

Using Bernstein’s inequality in Lemma
2.11
, we have

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\gamma k/8}\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{24/\gamma}}.

Then by Lemma
2.8
,

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{24/\gamma}}\lesssim t^{-3/2(1-2\gamma/24)}\|f_{l,k}\|_{L^{\frac{24}{24-\gamma}}}\lesssim 2^{-3M/2+\gamma M/8-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k+\gamma k/8}\epsilon_{1},

since
\alpha>1/2-\gamma/8
allows us to use Lemma
2.13
.

Another way to control
\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}
is to use Hölder’s inequality directly,

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\int e^{ix\cdot\xi}e^{-ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}\hat{f}_{l}(\xi)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\xi\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\int|\hat{f}_{l}(\xi)\psi_{k}(\xi)|d\xi

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{f}_{l}(\xi)\psi_{k}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}\|\chi_{2^{k-2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{k}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2^{\gamma k-2k_{+}+2k}\epsilon_{1}.

Combining the two results above gives (
3.10
).

If we use Bernstein’s inequality with a different parameter, Lemma
2.8
, and (
2.7
), we can also get the following bound

\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{k}\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{3}}\lesssim 2^{k-M/2}\|f_{l,k}\|_{L^{3/2}}\lesssim 2^{-M/2+3k/2}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{2}}.

We can obtain (
3.12
) in a similar fashion using Bernstein’s inequality, Lemma
2.8
, and Lemma
2.13
,

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\gamma k/8}\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{\frac{24}{8+\gamma}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\gamma k/8-M/2+\gamma M/8}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{\frac{24}{16-\gamma}}}\lesssim 2^{-M/2+\gamma M/8-k+\gamma k/4-2k_{+}+\gamma k}\epsilon_{1}.

Next, Lemma
2.8
and Lemma
2.13
give

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l,k}\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim t^{-3/4}\|f_{l,k}\|_{L^{4/3}}\lesssim 2^{-3M/4+k/4}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-3M/4-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k/4}\epsilon_{1},

for
t\in[2^{M-1},2^{M}]
.
∎

3.2
Time Derivatives Estimates

An important idea in this paper is to make use of the oscillation of
e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}
, where
\phi(\xi,\eta)=c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2}
is called the phase function. To capture the oscillation, we will integrate by parts using the identity

(\partial_{t}+\frac{P(\xi,\eta)}{t}\cdot\nabla_{\eta})e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}=iZ(\xi,\eta)e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}

where
P(\xi,\eta)
and
Z(\xi,\eta)
are polynomials. This introduces time derivatives inside the Duhamel’s term. Hence, we shall derive bounds for the time derivatives of
\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)
,
D^{\alpha}_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)
, and
\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)
in this section.

We will use the fact that
u_{l}(t,x)=e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}f_{l}(t,x)
is the solution to (
1.1
) such that
f_{l}\in\mathcal{C}([1,T];H^{10}\cap Z)
to find bounds for the time derivatives of
\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,x)
. In addition, we will do a further Littlewood-Paley decomposition on
f_{m}
and
f_{n}
in the nonlinear term.

Lemma 3.3
.

Under the assumption (
1.8
), the following estimate holds for any
t\in[2^{M-1},2^{M}]

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\min\{2^{-M-2k_{+}+\gamma k+k/2},2^{-10k_{+}-(1+\gamma/2)M}\}\epsilon_{1}^{2}

(3.14)

and

\displaystyle\|e^{ic_{l}t\Delta}\partial_{t}f_{l,k}(t,\xi)\|_{L^{6}}\lesssim 2^{-2M-2k_{+}+\gamma k-k/2}\epsilon_{1}^{2}.

(3.15)

Proof.

Assuming (
1.8
), the quadratic nonlinearity allows us to write the time derivative of the profile
f_{l}=e^{-ic_{l}t\Delta}u_{l}
as bilinear forms,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{f}_{l}(t,\xi)=

\displaystyle ic_{l}|\xi|^{2}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}\hat{u}_{l}(t,\xi)+e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}\partial_{t}\hat{u}_{l}(t,\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}\hat{u}_{m}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{u}_{n}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}=0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{u}_{m}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{u}_{n}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}=0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n}(t,\eta)d\eta.

Next, we perform dyadic decomposition on the frequency spaces of
f_{m}
and
f_{n}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{f}_{l}(t,\xi)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}=0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta.

By a change of variable with
\xi-\eta=\zeta
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\zeta|^{2}-c_{n}|\xi-\zeta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\zeta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\xi-\zeta)d\zeta

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}+c_{m}|\xi|^{2}-2c_{m}\xi\cdot\zeta)}q(\zeta,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\zeta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\xi-\zeta)d\zeta

The symmetry observed above allows us to reduce the problem to the cases when
k_{1}\geq k_{2}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{f}_{l}(t,\xi)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\bigg(\sum_{k_{1}\geq k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{k_{1}<k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta\bigg)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}=0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\bigg(\sum_{k_{1}\geq k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{k_{1}<k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0}A_{lmn}\sum_{k_{1}\geq k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}=0}A_{lmn}\sum_{k_{1}\geq k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta.

Truncating the difference by
\psi_{k}(\xi)
and taking

a=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0\\
c_{l}\neq c_{m}\end{subarray}}\{\big|2\log_{2}|c_{l}|\big|+3,2-\log_{2}|c_{m}/c_{n}+1|,10-\log_{2}|c_{m}/c_{l}-1|,3-\log_{2}(|c_{l}-c_{m}|/|c_{n}|)\},

(3.16)

we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)=

\displaystyle\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0}A_{lmn}\sum_{k_{1}\geq k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}=0}A_{lmn}\sum_{k_{1}\geq k_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\sum_{|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}=0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\sum_{|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0}A_{lmn}\sum_{k_{2}\leq k_{1}-a}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}=0}A_{lmn}\sum_{k_{2}\leq k_{1}-a}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a\\
k\leq k_{1}+a+2\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}=0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a\\
k\leq k_{1}+a+2\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0}A_{lmn}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{2}+a\leq k_{1}\\
|k-k_{1}|\leq 3\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta

\displaystyle+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}=0}A_{lmn}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{2}+a\leq k_{1}\\
|k-k_{1}|\leq 3\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta,

where
\chi^{1}_{k}=\{(k_{1},k_{2}):|k-k_{1}|\leq 3,k_{2}\leq k_{1}-a\}
,

\chi^{2}_{k}=\{(k_{1},k_{2}):|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a,k<k_{1}-a-2\}
and
\chi^{3}_{k}=\{(k_{1},k_{2}):|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a,|k-k_{1}|\leq a+2\}
.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{f}_{l,k}(t,\xi)=

\displaystyle\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0}A_{lmn}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi_{k}^{1}}F^{1}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}\neq 0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi_{k}^{2}\cup\chi_{k}^{3}}F^{1}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}

(3.17)

\displaystyle+\sum_{c_{m}+c_{n}=0}A_{lmn}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi^{1}_{k}}F^{2}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}+c_{n}=0\\
m\geq n\end{subarray}}A_{lmn}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi^{2}_{k}\cup\chi^{3}_{k}}F^{2}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}},

where

\displaystyle F^{1}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2})}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta\psi_{k}(\xi)

(3.18)

and

\displaystyle F^{2}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it(c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi|^{2}+2c_{m}\xi\cdot\eta)}q(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta.

(3.19)

By the bilinear estimate
L^{4}\times L^{4}\rightarrow L^{2}
in Lemma
2.3
and Lemma
2.4
,

\displaystyle\|\eqref{partialtf1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{ic_{l}t|\xi|^{2}}e^{-ic_{m}t|\xi-\eta|^{2}}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)e^{-ic_{n}t|\eta|^{2}}\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)\psi_{k}(\xi)d\eta\bigg\|_{L^{2}}

(3.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{4}}\|e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{4}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{4}}\|e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{4}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|f_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}.

Then, using the bounds in (
3.13
), (
3.2
), we get for any
t\in[2^{M-1},2^{M}]
,

\displaystyle\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi_{k}^{1}\cup\chi_{k}^{3}}\|\eqref{partialtf1}\|_{L^{2}}

(3.21)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi_{k}^{1}\cup\chi_{k}^{3}}\min\{\|e^{ic_{m}t\Delta}f_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{4}}\|e^{ic_{n}t\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{4}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|f_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi_{k}^{1}\cup\chi_{k}^{3}}2^{-2k_{1,+}-2k_{2,+}+\gamma k_{1}+\gamma k_{2}}\min\{2^{-3M/2-k_{1}/4-k_{2}/4},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2+k_{1}/2+k_{2}/2}\}\epsilon_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-2k_{+}+\gamma k-M+k/2}\epsilon_{1}^{2}.

Now, we shall show
\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi_{k}^{2}}\|\eqref{partialtf1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-m-2k_{+}+\gamma k+k/2}\epsilon_{1}
.

Recall that we defined
\phi(\xi,\eta)=c_{l}|\xi|^{2}-c_{m}|\xi-\eta|^{2}-c_{n}|\eta|^{2}.

When
(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi^{1}_{k}
, we know
k_{2}\leq k_{1}-a
, so
\psi_{k}(\xi)\psi_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\psi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\neq 0
implies

|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|=|2c_{m}(\xi-\eta)-2c_{n}\eta|\geq 2^{k_{1}+1-2}|c_{m}|-2^{k_{2}+1}|c_{n}|\geq|c_{m}|2^{k_{1}-2}\geq 2^{k_{1}-2-a}

and

|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2^{k_{1}+1}|c_{m}|+2^{k_{2}+1}|c_{n}|\leq|c_{m}|2^{k_{1}+2}\leq 2^{k_{1}+2+a},

if
c_{m}\neq-c_{n}
.
When
(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi^{2}_{k}
, we have
k\leq k_{1}-a-2
and
\psi_{k}(\xi)\psi_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\psi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\neq 0
implies

|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|\geq 2|c_{m}+c_{n}||\xi-\eta|-2|c_{n}||\xi|\geq|c_{n}|(2^{k_{1}+1-a}-2^{k+1})\geq|c_{n}|2^{k_{1}-a}\geq 2^{k_{1}-2a}

and

|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|\leq 2|c_{m}+c_{n}||\xi-\eta|+2|c_{n}||\xi|\leq 2^{k_{1}+1+a}+2^{k+1+a}\leq 2^{k_{1}+2+a}.

Therefore, when
(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi^{1}_{k}\cup\chi^{2}_{k}

\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta))\psi_{k}(\xi)\psi_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\psi_{k_{2}}(\eta)=\psi_{k}(\xi)\psi_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\psi_{k_{2}}(\eta).

(3.22)

So, we use the identity
\sum_{m}\frac{\partial_{\eta_{m}}\phi(\xi,\eta)}{it|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\partial_{\eta_{m}}e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}=e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}
to integrate by parts,

\displaystyle\eqref{partialtf1}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{\eta_{m}}e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\partial_{\eta_{m}}\phi(\xi,\eta)}{it|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta\psi_{k}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}\partial_{\eta_{m}}\frac{\partial_{\eta_{m}}\phi(\xi,\eta)}{it|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta\psi_{k}(\xi)

(3.23)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\partial_{\eta_{m}}\phi(\xi,\eta)}{it|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\partial_{\eta_{m}}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta\psi_{k}(\xi)

(3.24)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{it\phi(\xi,\eta)}\frac{\partial_{\eta_{m}}\phi(\xi,\eta)}{it|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}(t,\xi-\eta)\partial_{\eta_{m}}\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}(t,\eta)d\eta\psi_{k}(\xi).

(3.25)

Next, using the
L^{4}\times L^{4}\rightarrow L^{2}
bilinear estimate, Lemma
2.7
, Lemma
2.4
, (
3.2
) and (
3.13
), we have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{l2t.1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-m}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\partial_{\eta_{m}}\frac{\partial_{\eta_{m}}\phi(\xi,\eta)}{it|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta))\tilde{\psi}_{k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\times

\displaystyle\times\min\{\|e^{it\Delta}{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{4}}\|e^{it\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{4}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|e^{it\Delta}{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|e^{it\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-M-2k_{1}-2k_{1,+}+\gamma k_{1}-2k_{2,+}+\gamma k_{2}}\min\{2^{-3M/2-k_{1}/4-k_{2}/4},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2+k_{1}/2+k_{2}/2}\}\epsilon_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-2k_{1,+}+\gamma k_{1}-2k_{2,+}+\gamma k_{2}}\min\{2^{-5M/2-9k_{1}/4-k_{2}/4},2^{-M+3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2-3k_{1}/2+k_{2}/2}\}\epsilon_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-4k_{1,+}+2\gamma k_{1}}\min\{2^{-5M/2-5k_{1}/2},2^{-M+3k/2-k_{1}}\}\epsilon_{1}^{2},

since
(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi^{2}_{k}=\{|k_{1}-k_{2}|<a,\,k<k_{1}-a-2\}
. Then,
using the
L^{18/7}\times L^{9}\rightarrow L^{2}
bilinear estimate, Lemma
2.8
, Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
2.4
, we get

\displaystyle\|\eqref{l2t.2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\eqref{l2t.3}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-M}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\frac{\partial_{\eta_{m}}\phi(\xi,\eta)}{it|\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta)|^{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{k_{1}}(\nabla_{\eta}\phi(\xi,\eta))\tilde{\psi}_{n,k_{2}}(\eta)\|_{L^{1}}\times

\displaystyle\times\big(\min\{\|e^{it\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{18/7}}\|e^{it\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{9}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|e^{it\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|e^{it\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}

\displaystyle+\min\{\|e^{it\Delta}{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{9}}\|e^{it\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{18/7}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|e^{it\Delta}{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|e^{it\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-M-k_{1}}\big(\min\{\|e^{it\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{18/7}}\|e^{it\Delta}f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{9}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}

\displaystyle+\min\{\|e^{it\Delta}{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{9}}\|e^{it\Delta}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{18/7}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-M-k_{1}}\big(\min\{2^{-3M/2}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{18/11}}\|f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{9/8}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}

\displaystyle+\min\{2^{-3M/2}\|{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{9/8}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{18/11}},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2}\|{f}_{m,k_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla_{\xi}\hat{f}_{n,k_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\}\big),

where the
L^{p}
norms can be bounded by Lemma
2.13
, (
3.2
) and (
3.7
)

\displaystyle\|\eqref{l2t.2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\eqref{l2t.3}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-M-k_{1}-2k_{1,+}+\gamma k_{1}-2k_{2,+}+\gamma k_{2}}\big(\min\{2^{-3M/2-5k_{1}/6-2k_{2}/3},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2-k_{1}/2+k_{2}/2}\}

\displaystyle+\min\{2^{-3M/2-2k_{1}/3-5k_{2}/6},2^{3\min\{k,k_{2}\}/2+k_{1}/2-k_{2}/2}\}\big)\epsilon_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-4k_{1,+}+2\gamma k_{1}}\min\{2^{-5M/2-5k_{1}/2},2^{-M+3k/2-k_{1}}\}\epsilon_{1}^{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi_{k}^{2}}\|\eqref{partialtf1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\chi_{k}^{2}}2^{-4k_{1,+}+2\gamma k_{1}}\min\{2^{-5M/2-5k_{1}/2},2^{-M+3k/2-k_{1}}\}\epsilon_{1}^{2}

(3.26)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k_{1}\geq k}2^{-4k_{1,+}+2\gamma k_{1}}\min\{2^{-5M/2-5k_{1}/2},2^{-M+3k/2-k_{1}}\}\epsilon_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-M-2k_{+}+\gamma k+k/2}\epsilon_{1}^{2}.

Next, we work on the term
F^{2}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}
. By the bilinear estimate
